# Infant Food Puree Mixed Meat

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="infant-food-puree-mixed-meat-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="infant-food-puree-mixed-meat-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="infant-food-puree-mixed-meat-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Infant Food Puree Mixed Meat in the HMI library has 94 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn and Sr from 4 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a ## What counts as infant Food Puree Mixed Meat

<a id="hmi-prd-001"></a>
<a id="what-finished-product-category-formulations-formats-and-intended-uses-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-prd-002"></a>

<a id="hmi-prd-003"></a>
<a id="which-populations-and-exposure-routes-are-relevant-to-this-product"></a>

This page covers infant Food Puree Mixed Meat, as sold (94 results).<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy|3]]<span class="cite-card-journal">PLOS ONE</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/vella2019-malta-infant-foods-formulae-metals|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Vella C, Attard E · 2019</span><a source →</a></span></span></sup>

Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

## Infant Food Puree Mixed Meat overview

<a id="what-contamination-concerns-are-supported-for-this-product-category"></a>

Infant Food Puree Mixed Meat in the HMI library has 94 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn and Sr from 4 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Kirkpatrick DC, Conacher HBS, Méranger JC, Dabeka R, Collins B, McKenzie AD, Lacroix GMA, Savary G · 1980</span><a href="../sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy|3]]<span class="cite-card-journal">PLOS ONE</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup source →</a></span></span></sup>

Kirkpatrick et al. 1980 reported lead at 30 µg/kg (mean) and cadmium at 20 µg/kg (mean) in infant Food Puree Mixed Meat, as sold from Table 3 category average, all manufacturers.<sup class="cite">[[sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Kirkpatrick DC, Conacher HBS, Méranger JC, Dabeka R, Collins B, McKenzie AD, Lacroix GMA, Savary G · 1980</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods">The Trace Element Content of Canadian Baby Foods and Estimation of Trace Element Intake by Infants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Institute of Food Science and Technology Journal 13(4):154-161</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Meli et al. 2024 reported total mercury at 4 µg/kg (mean ± 0.9 SD) in infant Food Puree Mixed Meat, as sold from Homogenized baby food, meat.<sup class="cite">[[sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Meli MA, Desideri D, Sisti D, Fagiolino I, Roselli C · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy">Chemical characterization of baby food consumed in Italy</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">PLOS ONE</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the infant Food Puree Mixed Meat came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

50 rows can be rated against an HMI reference for the same form and basis, 0 rows have a direct same-study comparison, and the rest stay unrated with the reason recorded.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-infant-food-puree-mixed-meat" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-infant-food-puree-mixed-meat-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of infant food puree mixed meat, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Infant Food Puree Mixed Meat, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td><td>≤4</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>2 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Spungen et al. 2024</td><td>2</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for infant food puree mixed meat, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>Spungen et al. 2024 is the only compatible sampling family for infant food puree mixed meat, as sold on this basis in the completed infant food puree mixed meat ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium" class="internal" data-slug="sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium" data-no-cite-meta>Spungen et al. 2024</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div></details><div hidden data-form-reasons><p data-reason="bounds_span_categories" data-title="Non-detect spans the bands">The result Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Below the limit of detection</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI infant food puree mixed meat Pb reference · Infant Food Puree Mixed Meat, as sold. not detected as reported: ≤4 µg/kg. HMI median ≤4; Q1 ≤4; Q3 ≤4 µg/kg. 1 family. Too few reference units.</p><p>Only 2 reference unit is available; at least four are required by this descriptive display rule.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:infant-food-puree-mixed-meat-9ec7c747acf7:c0880a6f8356"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:infant-food-puree-mixed-meat-9ec7c747acf7:c0880a6f8356">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2018–2020</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>all samples</p><div data-selected-regional><p>All samples in the study.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>Microwave digestion, ICP-MS (Gray et al. 2020 method cited).</p><p>Below the limit of detection</p><p>TDS food no. 205; hybrid mean 2.0, lower bound 0, upper bound 4.0 µg/kg; reporting limit 4 µg/kg; 8 composite samples. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>1975</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>Table 3 category average, all manufacturers</p><div data-selected-regional><p>All samples in the study.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>AAS (Varian Techtron AA-120 / Perkin Elmer 403); dry ashing at 450 C and nitric acid for most elements; Pb by HPB method FA-67 (wet digestion, SrSO4 coprecipitation).</p><p>No censoring reported</p><p>Mean on as-consumed basis, weighted by production volume, not recovery-corrected; values below detection limit set equal to the limit when averaging, so means are overestimates. <a id="testing-infant-food-puree-mixed-meat-for-metals"></a>
<a id="sampling-testing-and-verification"></a>
## Testing infant Food Puree Mixed Meat for metals

<a id="hmi-prd-022"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-needed-for-lot-decisions-surveillance-and-verification-of-a-process-change"></a>
<a id="hmi-prd-023"></a>
<a id="which-analytical-or-migrationrelease-methods-match-this-product-species-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-prd-024"></a>
<a id="what-can-testing-and-supplier-coas-establish-about-a-lot-and-what-cannot-be-generalized-to-all-production"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 4 µg/kg (Spungen et al. 2024) to 30 µg/kg (Kirkpatrick et al. 1980), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Kirkpatrick DC, Conacher HBS, Méranger JC, Dabeka R, Collins B, McKenzie AD, Lacroix GMA, Savary G · 1980</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods">The Trace Element Content of Canadian Baby Foods and Estimation of Trace Element Intake by Infants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Institute of Food Science and Technology Journal 13(4):154-161</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium ranged from 1 µg/kg (Spungen et al. 2024) to 10 µg/kg (Kirkpatrick et al. 1980), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Kirkpatrick DC, Conacher HBS, Méranger JC, Dabeka R, Collins B, McKenzie AD, Lacroix GMA, Savary G · 1980</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods">The Trace Element Content of Canadian Baby Foods and Estimation of Trace Element Intake by Infants</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Canadian Institute of Food Science and Technology Journal 13(4):154-161</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/kirkpatrick1980-trace-elements-canadian-baby-foods">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for total arsenic ranged from 16 µg/kg (Meli et al. 2024) to 19 µg/kg (Meli et al. 2024), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Meli MA, Desideri D, Sisti D, Fagiolino I, Roselli C · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy">Chemical characterization of baby food consumed in Italy</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">PLOS ONE</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/meli2024-chemical-characterization-baby-food-italy">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

<a id="what-supports-the-certainty-judgments-what-remains-unresolved-and-when-and-by-whom-were-answers-reviewed"></a>

Every source record that names infant Food Puree Mixed Meat was decided on its content: 4 occurrence extracted, 4 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.

<!-- END: hmi-contradiction-watch -->

## Infant Food Puree Mixed Meat FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-infant-food-puree-mixed-meat" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-infant-food-puree-mixed-meat" data-question="What heavy metals are found in infant Food Puree Mixed Meat?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in infant Food Puree Mixed Meat?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, aluminium, tin, antimony, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn and Sr in infant Food Puree Mixed Meat, most often lead, cadmium and
